### Q22. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A stone of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> tied to a light inextensible string of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is whirling in a circular path of the radius <math><mi>L</mi></math> in the vertical plane. If the ratio of the maximum tension to the minimum tension in the string is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>. What is the speed of stone (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) at the highest point of the circle? (Taking <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>T</mi><mtext>max</mtext></msub><msub><mi>T</mi><mtext>min</mtext></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><msup><mtext mathvariant="italic">mv</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mtext mathvariant="italic">L</mtext></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext></mrow><mrow><mfrac><msup><mtext mathvariant="italic">mv</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mtext mathvariant="italic">L</mtext></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

Solving it.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mtext mathvariant="italic">mv</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mtext mathvariant="italic">L</mtext></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">v</mtext><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn><mtext mathvariant="italic">gL</mtext></msqrt></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mtext>m s</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q23. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

<p>In a rotor, a hollow vertical cylindrical structure rotates about its axis and a person rests against the inner wall. At a particular speed, the floor below the person is removed and the person hangs resting against the wall without any floor. If the radius of the rotor is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and the coefficient of static friction between the wall and the person is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, find the minimum speed (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) of any point the wall at which the floor may be removed. Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

The situation is shown in the diagram

<strong><u><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014033_AnsExp1_1.jpg" /></u></strong>

When the floor is removed, the forces on the person are

(1) weight mg downward

(2) normal force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> due to the wall, towards the centre

(3) frictional force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub></math>, parallel to the wall, upward.

The person is moving in a circle with a uniform speed, so its acceleration is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac bevelled="true"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mfrac></math> towards the centre.

Newton's law for the horizontal direction (2nd law) and for the vertical direction (1st law) give

                      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi>mv</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></math>                        ...(i)

and                <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mi>mg</mi></math>.                            ...(ii)

​For the minimum speed when the floor may be removed, the friction is limiting one and so equals μ<sub>s</sub>N. This gives

                   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mi>mg</mi></math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>or,</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>μ</mtext> <mtext>s</mtext> </msub> <msup> <mtext>mv</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>using (i)</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>or,</mtext><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mtext>v</mtext><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mtext>rg</mtext><msub><mtext>μ</mtext><mtext>s</mtext></msub></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mtext>2 m</mtext><mo>×</mo><msup><mtext>10 m/s</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mtext>0.2</mtext></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msup><mtext>10 m s</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q24. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A rough platform <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>OA</mi></math> rotates in a vertical plane about a horizontal axis through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> with a constant counterclockwise velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. As it passes through the position <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, a small mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is placed upon it at a radial distance. If the mass is observed to slip at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>37</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>, then the coefficient of friction between the mass &amp; the member is


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image10-5352abe7-db3d-482c-ba75-161eda523efe.jpg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

As the mass is at the verge of slipping

 <math><mo>∴</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mn>37</mn></mrow></mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mn>37</mn></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></math> 

 <math><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4.5</mn></math> 

 <math><mo>∴</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q25. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>An unbanked circular highway curve on the level ground makes a turn of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>90</mn><mo>°</mo></math>. The highway carries traffic at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>108</mn><mo> </mo><mi>km</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, and the centripetal force on a vehicle is not to exceed <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> of its weight. What is the approximate minimum length of the curve, in km?</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>41</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>km</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 126px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>km</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 109px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 109px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>km</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 72px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>41</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>km</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 126px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>0</mn><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>g</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mtext>108</mtext><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mn>0</mn><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>m</mtext></math>,

Length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mtext>1.413 km</mtext></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q26. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A car moves uniformly along a horizontal sine curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>sin</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>α</mi><mo>)</mo></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>α</mi></math> are certain constants. The coefficient of friction between the wheels and the road is equal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi></math>. The maximum constant speed at which the car can travel without sliding is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">kg</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">a</mtext></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">kg</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">a</mtext></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">kg</mtext><msqrt><mfrac><mi>&#945;</mi><mtext mathvariant="italic">a</mtext></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">kg</mtext><msqrt><mfrac><mi>&#945;</mi><mtext mathvariant="italic">a</mtext></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">kg</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">a</mtext></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<b>Step I :</b> Determine the radius of curvature of given curve at point P (x, y).

∵       <math> <mrow> <mi>y</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mi>a</mi> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>sin</mtext> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>&#945;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mo>&#8756;</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mi>y</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>&#945;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>&#945;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

&there4;      <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mi>y</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mi>&#945;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>sin</mtext> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>&#945;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

&there4;    the radius of curvature is  <math> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mi>y</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>/</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mi>y</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

                                             <math> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mi>a</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mi>&#945;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>/</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mi>&#945;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext>sin</mtext> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>&#945;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>        ...(i)

<b>Step II</b>: Relate the friction and centripetal acceleration. In this case, centripetal acceleration is provided by force of friction.

∵                              <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8804;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>L</mi></msub></math>     or<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>f</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8804;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></math>

or                              <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>mv</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>&#8804;</mo> <mtext>kmg</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>or</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>v</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>&#8804;</mo> <mtext>kg</mtext> </mrow> </math>

&there4;                              <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msubsup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>v</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mtext>max</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mtext>min</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mi>k</mi> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </math>

&there4;                              <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>v</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mtext>max</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mi>k</mi> <mtext>g</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mtext>min</mtext> </msub> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

From equation (i), for the minimum value of the radius of curvature,

                              <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>&#945;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>&#960;</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

&there4;                              <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mtext>min</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mi>&#945;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>                             (from equation. (i))

&there4;                              <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>v</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mtext>max</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>k</mi> <mtext>g</mtext> <msup> <mi>&#945;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> <mo>=</mo> <mi>&#945;</mi> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>k</mi> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q27. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A ring of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> and of the radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is making <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo> </mo><mi>rpm</mi></math> about an axis through its centre perpendicular to its plane. The tension (in newtons) developed in the ring is:

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **250**

**Detailed Solution:**

As  <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>t</mtext> <mo>sin</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>&#952;</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>dm</mtext> <msup> <mi>&#969;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> </math>  (for small angle sin <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>&#952;</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>&#8594;</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>&#952;</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>)

but <math> <mrow> <mtext>dm</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>&#8467;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> <mi>&#952;</mi> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> </math>

As <math> <mrow> <mi>&#8467;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mi>&#960;</mi> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> <msup> <mi>&#969;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>&#960;</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>


            <u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0027030_Ans_.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 300px; height: 160px;" /></strong></u>


Put <math> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mi>&#960;</mi> <mo>kg</mo> <mtext>,&#160;</mtext> <mi>&#969;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mi>&#960;</mi> </mrow> </math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mn>5</mn> <mn>0&#160;</mn><mtext>N</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q28. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A car is moving in a circular horizontal track of radius <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mtext>m</mtext></math> A plumb bob is suspended from the roof of the car by a light rigid rod. The angle made by the rod with the vertical is <math><msup><mrow><mn>60</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> Then the car moves with a constant speed of _______ <math><mtext>m</mtext><mo>/</mo><mtext>s</mtext><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mtext>g</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>10 </mn><mi></mi><mtext>m</mtext><mo>/</mo><msup><mrow><mtext>s</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/0e6db851-39b5-448b-ba22-db220d12e8c3.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width:1.79375in;height:1.92014in" />
<math><mtext>T</mtext><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mtext>mv</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mtext>R</mtext></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mtext>T</mtext><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mtext>mg</mtext></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mtext>v</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mtext>Rg</mtext></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>×</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>×</mo><mi>g</mi></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>60</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>10</mn></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>10</mn></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>36</mn></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6 </mn><mi></mi><mtext>m</mtext><mo>/</mo><mtext>s</mtext></math>

</details>

### Q29. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the linear velocity if the angular velocity vector is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>ω</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext> </mtext><mn>3</mn><mtext> </mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>−</mo><mn>4</mn><mtext> </mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mtext> </mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>  and position vector is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext> </mtext><mn>5</mn><mtext> </mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>−</mo><mn>6</mn><mtext> </mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mtext> </mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>18</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>13</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>18</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>13</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

As we know that

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>&#215;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>4</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>&#215;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>(</mo><mn>5</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>6</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><mo>&#8756;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi>w</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>|</mo><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>4</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>5</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>6</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>6</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable><mo>|</mo></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>or</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi>w</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>18</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>18</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>18</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>13</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

</details>

### Q30. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The figure below shows the top view of a hollow cylindrical room having a frictionless floor. A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is projected with a speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math>, on the smooth horizontal floor, along the wall of the room. The average normal force exerted by the cylindrical walls, on the block, during its motion from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/3eeede.JPG" style="width: 120px; height: 154px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>π</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>π</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>π</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The change in momentum of the block from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is</p><p><math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi></math></p><p>The time it takes to move from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mi>v</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>The average force exerted by the wall on the block is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>avg</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q31. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A small block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> slides along a smooth frictional track as shown in the figure. If it starts from rest at <math><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>, what is the resultant force acting on it at <math><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></math>?

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/image__6_.png" style="width: 300px; height: 173px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>70</mn></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">mg</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>60</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">mg</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>75</mn></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">mg</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><msqrt><mn>65</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">mg</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><msqrt><mn>65</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">mg</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/dddere_phys.png" style="width: 250px; height: 124px;" />

From energy conservation,

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msqrt></math>

At <math><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>

So net force at <math><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>65 </mn></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q32. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A pendulum has an angular amplitude <math><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></math>. The tension in the string at extreme position is T<sub>1</sub> and at the bottom is T<sub>2</sub>. If it is known that T<sub>2</sub> = 2T<sub>1</sub>, then what is the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>θ</mi></math>?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **0.75**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mg cos</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>       ..... (1)

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>mv</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>gh</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

             <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext> </mrow> </math>

             <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext mathvariant="italic">l</mtext> </mrow> </math>

or   <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>    ....... (2)

<u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0021690_AnsExp_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 273px; height: 193px;" /></strong></u>

Given T<sub>2</sub> = 2T<sub>1</sub>

or  <math> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>2mg cos</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

or  <math> <mrow> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
                = 0.75

</details>

---

### Q33. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is fastened to one end of a string and another particle of mass <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> is attached to the middle point, the other end of the string being fastened to a fixed point on a smooth horizontal table. If the particles are made to revolve in a horizontal circular path as shown in the figure, then the ratio of tension in the part of the string between the centre and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> to the tension in the part of the string between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image63-33074a96-2d43-45a9-8ef2-542fa7377990.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="height: 148px; width: 150px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image64-b75188f4-e5a0-4f05-a69e-cf3a219233eb.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />



for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo></math></p><p>        <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> ......(i)

for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo></math></p><p>       <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></math> ....(ii)</p><p>Dividing both the equations:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q34. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A particle is moving in a circle of radius <math><mi>R</mi></math> in such a way that at any instant the tangential retardation of the particle and the normal acceleration of the particle are equal. If its speed at <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>, the time taken to complete the first revolution is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

When a particle moves in a circular motion, it is acted upon by centripetal force directed towards the centre. Hence, centripetal acceleration is

<math><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mrow><msubsup><mo stretchy="false">&#8747;</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></msubsup><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mrow><msubsup><mo stretchy="false">&#8747;</mo><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow></msubsup><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>R</mi><msubsup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow></msubsup></math>

<math><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></msubsup><mi>d</mi><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>R</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mi>T</mi></msubsup><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>&#10233;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi></math>


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image2-2c072209-00f0-4e20-a427-ed1c25941f61.jpg" style="width: 103px; height: 136px;" />

</details>

---

### Q35. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A hollow vertical cylinder of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> and height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math> has a smooth internal surface. A small particle is held in contact with the inner side of the upper rim at a point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>. It is given a horizontal speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> tangential to the rim. It leaves the lower rim at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></math>, vertically below <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>. The number of revolutions made by the particle will be [Take acceleration due to gravity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi></math>]

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>h</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>h</mi></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mi>h</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>h</mi></mrow><mi>g</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>π</mi></mrow><mi>g</mi></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>h</mi></mrow><mi>g</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

A hollow vertical

<math> <mrow> <mtext>h</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>gt</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>n</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>π</mi> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mtext>t</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>n</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>π</mi> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <msqrt> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>h</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q36. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

For the arrangement in the figure, the particle <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> attached to one end of a string which moves on a horizontal table in a circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> (where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi></math> is the length of the string) with the constant angular speed <math><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi></math>. The other end of the string attached to mass <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> which rests on a vertical rod. When the rod collapse, the acceleration of mass <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> at that instant

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image8-ca32010f-92d5-4bd0-a566-791efe2ab259.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="height: 263px; width: 250px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>l</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>l</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>l</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>l</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

From Newton&#39;s second law


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image9-36f32d3b-300d-4a85-8379-e16c2855451f.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mfrac><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>

And <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>

After solving above equations, we get

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>l</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q37. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle is moving in a uniform circular motion on a horizontal surface. Particle position and velocity at time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> are shown in the figure in the coordinate system. Which of the indicated variable on the vertical axis is incorrectly matched by the graph shown alongside for particle's motion-

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Question_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 185px; height: 152px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** y component of force keeping particle moving in a circle

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Answer_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 201px; height: 142px;" />
- **(B)** x component of velocity

<math> </math><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Answer_3.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 199px; height: 140px;" />
- **(C)** Angular velocity of the particle

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Answer_2.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 199px; height: 141px;" />
- **(D)** x coordinate of the particle

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Answer_4.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 197px; height: 142px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) x component of velocity

<math> </math><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Answer_3.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 199px; height: 140px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/8585415841.JPG" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 200px; height: 208px;" />

where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>cos</mi><mi>θ</mi></math>, and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>sin</mi><mi>θ</mi></math>

at time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi></math>, in Uniform circular motion only centripetal force exists constant in magnitude

So, Y - component of force is <math> <mo>⇒</mo><mover accent="true"> <mrow> <msub><mtext>F</mtext><mtext>y</mtext></msub> </mrow> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mtext>F</mtext><mtext>sin</mtext><mtext>θ</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </math>

<math> <msub><mtext>F</mtext><mtext>y</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mtext>F</mtext><mtext>sin</mtext><mtext>ω</mtext><mtext>t</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </math> (as, <!-- MathType@Translator@5@5@MathML2 (namespace attr).tdl@MathML 2.0 (namespace attr)@ --> <math> <mrow> <mtext>θ</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext>ωt</mtext></mrow> </math> <!-- MathType@End@5@5@ --> )

So given graph is correct for <math><msub><mtext>F</mtext><mtext>y</mtext></msub></math> vs <math><mtext>t</mtext></math>

In Uniform circular motion. <math> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mtext>ω</mtext> <mtext>r</mtext> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> </mrow> </math>  constant

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/59654964196.JPG" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 200px; height: 215px;" />

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/416418541.JPG" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 200px; height: 190px;" />

<math> <mo>⇒</mo><mover accent="true"> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mtext>x</mtext> </msub> </mrow> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mtext>V</mtext><mtext>sin</mtext><mtext>ω</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>t</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover></math>

So given graph for <math> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mtext>x</mtext> </msub> <mo>−</mo><mtext>t</mtext> </math> is incorrect.

In Uniform circular motion.,   <math> <mrow> <mtext>ω</mtext><mo>=</mo> </mrow> </math> constant

<math> <mrow> <mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>ω</mtext><mo>−</mo><mtext>t</mtext> </mrow> </math>   graph is correct

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/8418541796.JPG" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 200px; height: 173px;" />

<math> <mo>∵</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>x</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext>r</mtext><mtext>cos</mtext><mtext>θ</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>r</mtext><mtext>cos</mtext><mtext>ω</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>t</mtext> </math>

<math> <mo>⇒</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>x</mtext><mo>−</mo><mtext>t</mtext> </math>  graph is correct

</details>

### Q38. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A small block slides with velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msqrt><mi>g</mi><mi>r</mi></msqrt></math> on the horizontal frictionless surface as shown in the figure. The block leaves the surface at a point <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. What is the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cosθ</mi></math>?

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image61-75d74bb2-1e50-416d-9efa-4998af400632.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="height: 123px; width: 200px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **0.6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image62-a2edc3c9-9d0b-4c35-a13d-69b472904c71.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />



<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> .....(i)

From energy conservation

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi></math> ...(ii)

From (i) and (ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></math>

</details>

### Q39. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A bead of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> can slide without friction along a vertical ring of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math>. One end of a spring of force constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math> is connected to the bead and the other end is fixed at the centre of the ring. Initially, the bead is at the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and due to a small push it starts sliding down the ring. If the bead momentarily loses contact with the ring at the instant when the spring makes an angle of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>°</mo></math> with the vertical, then the natural length of the spring is


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image9-110c4690-0d8a-43c5-886a-45aee07daa51.jpg" style="width: 200px; height: 115px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>9</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image10-b66191ba-b9cb-47c3-af38-d1c57da92dc1.jpg" style="height: 119px; width: 200px;" /></p><p>Let's say the bead loses contact with the ring at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo mathvariant="italic"> </mo><mi>cos</mi><mn>60</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>Applying the conservation of mechanical energy principle between <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> &amp; <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>cos</mi><mn>60</mn><mo>°</mo></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>The natural length of the spring <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q40. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A boat is travelling with a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>27</mn><mo> </mo><mi>km</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> due east. An observer is situated at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> south of the line of travel. The angular velocity of the boat relative to the observer in the position shown will be 

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/WhatsApp_Image_2020-07-23_at_3.48.15_PM.jpeg" style="width: 200px; height: 142px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>125</mn><mo> </mo><mi>rad</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 1px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** Zero
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 56px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>67</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 119px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>125</mn><mo> </mo><mi>rad</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 1px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mi>v</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>27</mn><mo> </mo><mi>km</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Let us assume the location of the observer as the origin

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mtext mathvariant="italic">r</mtext><mo mathvariant="italic">→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mn>0</mn><mtext> tan </mtext><mi>θ</mi><mover><mtext>i</mtext><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mn>0</mn><mover><mtext>j</mtext><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mtext mathvariant="italic">V</mtext><mo mathvariant="italic">→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mover><mtext mathvariant="italic">r</mtext><mo mathvariant="italic">→</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">t</mtext></mrow></mfrac><mo> ; </mo><mtext>7.5</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mn>0</mn><msup><mtext> sec</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mi> θ</mi><mfrac><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mi>θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">t</mtext></mrow></mfrac><mover><mtext>i</mtext><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>7.5</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mn>0</mn><mi>ω</mi><msup><mtext> sec</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>θ</mi><mo> </mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><mo> </mo><mtext>7.5</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mn>0</mn><mi>ω</mi><msup><mtext> sec</mtext><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mn>5</mn><mo>°</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mtext>7.5</mtext><mrow><mn>3</mn><mn>0</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mtext>7.5</mtext><mrow><mn>6</mn><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mtext>0.125 </mtext><mi>rad</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>
