### Q41. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The velocity and acceleration of a point-like body at the initial moment of its motion are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo> </mo></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> respectively. The acceleration vector of the body remains constant. The minimum radius of curvature of the trajectory of the body is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/0021277_queCA_1.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 150px; height: 62px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The acceleration vector shall change the component of velocity u<sub>II</sub> along the acceleration vector.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msub><mi>a</mi><mtext>n</mtext></msub></mfrac></math>

The radius of curvature <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>r</mi><mrow><mi>min</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub></math>means v is minimum and a<sub>n</sub> is maximum. This is at the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> when the component of velocity parallel to the acceleration vector becomes zero, that is u<sub>II</sub> = 0.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0021277_AnsExp_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 313px; height: 203px;" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msubsup><mi>u</mi><mo>&#8869;</mo><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mi>a</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mn>4</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mtext>&#160;m</mtext></math>

</details>

---

### Q42. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A simple pendulum consisting of a mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math> attached to a string of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> is released from rest at an angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">α</mi></math>. A pin is located at a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi></math> below the pivot point. When the pendulum swings down, the string hits the pin as shown in the figure. If the particle doesn't undergo a complete vertical circle, then the maximum angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo> </mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&lt;</mo><mfrac bevelled="true"><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> through which the string  swings after hitting the pin is


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image18-6e54d7d5-2518-4887-a771-07b7585fb11f.jpg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>⁡</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">α</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>⁡</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">α</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8289;</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>⁡</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">α</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

At the bottom most point, square of speed of bob,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>–</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">α</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

It will rise further to a height,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>L</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">α</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>L</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">α</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>⁡</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">α</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>

</details>

### Q43. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The mass of a pendulum bob is m = 2 kg and the length of the string is <math><mi>l</mi></math>. It is displaced through <math><mn>90</mn><mo>°</mo></math> from the vertical and released. What minimum tension (in N) should the string should be able to withstand so that it doesn't break during motion?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **60**

**Detailed Solution:**

Velocity at the lowest point, <math><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi></msqrt></math>

At the lowest point, the tension in the string

<math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi> = 60 N </mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q44. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The angular velocity of a particle is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>ω</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> about the origin. If the position vector of the particle is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>, then its linear velocity is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>14</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

</details>

### Q45. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A stone tied to a string is rotated in a vertical circle. The minimum speed of the stone during a complete vertical circular motion

**Options:**

- **(A)** is independent of the mass of the stone
- **(B)** is independent of the length of the string
- **(C)** decreases with increasing mass of the stone
- **(D)** decreases with increasing length of the string

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) is independent of the mass of the stone**

**Detailed Solution:**

<div><p align="left" style="text-align:left;"><span>Minimum speed with which the string is rotating in a vertical circle, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>)</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><msqrt><mi>gr</mi></msqrt></math> </span></p><p align="left" style="text-align:left;"><span>i</span><i><span>.</span></i><span>e</span><i><span>,</span></i><span> the minimum speed of stone is independent of the mass of stone.</span></p></div>

</details>

---

### Q46. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> moves along a circle of the radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>π</mi></mfrac><mo> </mo><mi>cm</mi></math> with a constant tangential acceleration. What is the magnitude of this acceleration (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) if the kinetic energy of the particle becomes equal to <math><mn>8</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> by the end of the second revolution after the beginning of the motion? (Particle starts from rest)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

Tangential acceleration <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo></math> constant <math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

At the end of the second revolution, angular velocity is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi></math> then

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#945;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><msup><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

K.E. of the particle is <math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q47. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/599394A.JPG" style="width: 160px; height: 150px;" />
As shown in the figure, a bob of mass <math><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is tied to a massless string whose other end portion is wound on a fly wheel (disc) of radius <math><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></math> and mass <math><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>.</mo></math> When released from rest the bob starts falling vertically. When it has covered a distance of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi></math> the angular speed of the wheel will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>gh</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>gh</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>gh</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msqrt><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>gh</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>gh</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Using work-energy theorem </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>mgh</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>mv</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>Iω</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>mgh</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>mω</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfrac><msup><mi>mr</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>    ( since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>mr</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>rω</mi></math> )</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>mgh</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>mω</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>gh</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>gh</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q48. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A small block of mass <math> <mtext>m</mtext> </math> is released from rest from point <math> <mtext>A</mtext> </math> inside a smooth hemisphere bowl of radius <math> <mtext>R</mtext> </math>, which is fixed on ground such that <math> <mrow> <mtext>OA</mtext></mrow> </math> is horizontal. The ratio <math> <mrow> <mtext>(x)</mtext></mrow> </math> of magnitude of centripetal force and normal reaction on the block at any point <math> <mtext>B</mtext> </math> varies with <math><mi>θ</mi></math> as:

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/image13-05f109c8-035f-43b8-bd1c-910a6870.jpg" style="width: 237px; height: 194px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image14-b0b3cd15-8ffc-442e-a4f5-8425c2eb42de.jpg" />
- **(B)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image15-65b82a1f-ad7e-4452-a724-90752326f52d.jpg" />
- **(C)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image16-16b93712-e277-48fc-9768-88ccebf1dd32.jpg" />
- **(D)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image17-31d6e97b-86bd-4872-931b-a1ec360efa9e.jpg" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image14-b0b3cd15-8ffc-442e-a4f5-8425c2eb42de.jpg" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>N</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image18-ff248e38-07e6-4f3a-8491-b32ea2a1c4a3.jpg" />

By energy conservation,

<math><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi><mi> </mi><mi>s</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mi>R</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>o</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> (constant)

<math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q49. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi></math> is a light rigid rod, which is rotating about a vertical axis passing through end  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> . A spring of force constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi></math> and natural length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi></math>  is attached at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and its other end is attached to a small bead of mass  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math>. The bead can slide without friction on the rod. At the initial moment, the bead is at rest (w.r.t the rod) and the spring is unstretched. Select incorrect options :</p> <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0032416_Question.jpg" style="width: 212px; height: 151px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>max</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#x3C9;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#x3C9;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>max</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#x3C9;</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><msup><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#x3C9;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>max</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#x3C9;</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><msup><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#x3C9;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>max</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#x3C9;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#x3C9;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>max</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#x3C9;</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><msup><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#x3C9;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Velocity will be maximum at the equilibrium position

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>

Now, using work-energy theorem
    <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/32416_figure.png" style="width: 204px; height: 157px;" />

<math><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo></math> Work done by all the forces

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mtext>max</mtext><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mi>x</mi></msubsup><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>d</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mtext>max</mtext><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>l</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>m</mi></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mtext>max</mtext><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>x</mi></mrow><mi>m</mi></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>l</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mi>m</mi></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mtext>max</mtext><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>l</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><msup><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>max</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><msup><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

</details>

### Q50. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is placed on a vertical fixed circular track and then it is given velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math> along the track at position <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> on track. The coefficient of friction between the block and the track varies with the angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>θ</mi></math>. If the block moves on track with constant speed then the coefficient of friction is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/640321_que.JPG" style="width: 200px; height: 114px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cot</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#x3BC;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>tan</mi><mi>&#x3B8;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi>&#x3B8;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mi>R</mi><mtext>&#x2009;</mtext><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#x3BC;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#x3B8;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>tan</mi><mi>&#x3B8;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mi>R</mi><mtext>&#x2009;</mtext><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Since the block is moving with a constant speed, at any instant its tangential acceleration is zero. Hence,</p><p><math><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math></p><p>Also,</p><p><math><mi>N</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math></p><p><math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q51. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In figure, a particle is placed at the highest point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> of a smooth sphere of radius <math><mi>r</mi></math>. It is given slight push, and it leaves the sphere at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>, at a depth <math><mi>h</mi><mi> </mi></math>vertically below <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>. The value of <math><mi>h</mi></math> is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/48519_que.JPG" style="width: 150px; height: 169px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>r</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>r</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>r</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>r</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

If <math><mi>v</mi></math> velocity acquired at <math><mi>B</mi></math>, then

<math><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>

The particle will leave the sphere at <math><mi>B</mi></math>, when

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>mv</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mgcos</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>gh</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> <mtext>.</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>r</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>h</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/WhatsApp_Image_2020-07-02_at_3.16.37_PM.jpeg" style="width: 200px; height: 184px;" />

Which gives <math><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q52. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Tangential acceleration of a particle moving in a circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> varies with time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi></math> as shown in figure (initial velocity of the particle is zero). Time after which total acceleration of particle makes an angle of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>°</mo></math> with radial acceleration is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0028217_correct_xyz.jpg" style="width: 153px; height: 138px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 77px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 116px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>2</mn><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 59px; top: 41px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(D)** <math><mrow>  <msqrt>    <mn>2</mn>  </msqrt></mrow></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>2</mn><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 59px; top: 41px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given that  <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>a</mtext> <mtext>t</mtext> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>t</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <msup> <mtext>tan 60</mtext> <mtext>o</mtext> </msup> </mrow> </math>   </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mtext>a</mtext><mtext>t</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mtext>t</mtext><mo>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#8230;</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math>

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>dv</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>dt</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> <mtext>t</mtext> </mrow> </math>                                </p><p><math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo> <munderover> <mo>&#8747;</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>v</mtext> </munderover> <mtext>dv</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> <mtext>t dt</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0028217_Ans_._.Xyz.jpg" style="width: 235px; height: 255px;" />

<math> <mrow> <mtext>v</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> <msup> <mtext>t</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>            ...(2)

Given that   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mtext>tan 30</mtext><mtext>o</mtext></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mtext>a</mtext><mtext>t</mtext></msub><msub><mtext>a</mtext><mtext>c</mtext></msub></mfrac><mo>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#8658;&#160;&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mtext>a</mtext><mtext>t</mtext></msub><msub><mtext>a</mtext><mtext>c</mtext></msub></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;</mo><msub><mtext>a</mtext><mtext>c</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><msub><mtext>a</mtext><mtext>t</mtext></msub></math>

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>v</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>R</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> <msub> <mtext>a</mtext> <mtext>t</mtext> </msub> <mo>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#8230;</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

From (1) and (2)  <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>t</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> <mo>&#215;</mo> <msqrt> <mn>3</mn> </msqrt> <mtext>t</mtext> <mo>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msup> <mtext>t</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mtext>t</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>t</mtext> <mn>3</mn> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </math>                </p><p><math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;</mo> <mtext>t</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msup> <mn>2</mn> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>/</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mtext>sec.</mtext> </mrow> </math></p>

</details>

### Q53. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A light rod of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> is hanging from the vertical smooth wall of a vehicle moving with acceleration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>g</mi></math>along the horizontal plane having a small mass attached at its one end and is free to rotate about an axis passing through the other end. The minimum velocity given to the mass at its equilibrium position with respect to the vehicle so that it can complete vertical circular motion is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>8</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>8</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0067155.png" style="width: 143px; height: 135px;" /></p><p>The effective acceleration due to gravity in the reference frame of the vehicle is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>g</mi><mrow><mi>e</mi><mi>f</mi><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mi>g</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></math></p><p>The angle made by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>g</mi><mrow><mi>e</mi><mi>f</mi><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub></math> with the verticle is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>g</mi></mrow><mi>g</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math></p><p>At the lowest point (shown in the figure), let the speed is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math>, then</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn><mi>L</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>8</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></msqrt></math>

         </p>

</details>

---

### Q54. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An aircraft loops a vertical loop of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>500</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> with a constant velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>360</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi>kmh</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Find the weight of the flier of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>70</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> in the lower, upper, and middle points of the loop.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi></math>
- **(D)** none of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Forces on the flier in the frame of aircaraft (non inertial reference frame) is:

(i) Normal reaction (N)

(ii)Pseudo force <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>m</mi> <msup> <mrow> <mi>v</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

(iii) Gravitational force d(mg)

According to problem,

                        v<sub>T</sub> = v<sub>b</sub> = v<sub>m</sub> = v

The net force on flier at every point should be zero in the frame of aircraft. For top point T,

                             <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi>N</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>T</mi> </mrow> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msubsup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>mv</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mo>∴</mo> <msub> <mrow> <mi>N</mi> </mrow> <mrow><mi>T</mi> </mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mi>mv</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </math>

                             = 0.7 kN

For bottom point,<math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>B</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msubsup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>mv</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mi>b</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>mv</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

                             = 2.1 kN.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014518_AnsExp_1_1.jpg" style="width: 214px; height: 219px;" />

For middle point,<math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi>N</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>m</mi> </mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>mv</mtext> <mrow> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>R</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </msqrt> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>5</mn> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mi>k</mi> <mi></mi><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q55. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> car takes a round turn of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> with a velocity of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>36</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The centripetal force is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: -20px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>750</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Centripetal force in a circular motion is given as

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>500</mn><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mn>10</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>50</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q56. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A small bead of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is free to move on a circular hoop. The circular hoop has centre at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> and radius<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and it rotates about a fixed vertical axis. The coefficient of friction between bead and hoop is <math><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.5</mn></math> . The maximum angular speed of the hoop for which the bead does not have relative motion with respect to the hoop, at the position shown in the figure is: (Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> )

<img alt="" height="234" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image2-b93ca5d7-37ee-4317-996b-e9715da5fea1.jpg" style="max-width:"100%"; object-fit:contain" width="139" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>10</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>15</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>30</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>30</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The maximum angular speed of the hoop corresponds to the situation when the bead is just about to slide upwards.
The free-body diagram of the bead is

<img alt="" height="166" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image3-b1c22648-d561-4333-95d8-db75257ec90a.jpg" style="max-width:"100%"; object-fit:contain" width="394" />

For the bead not to slide upwards

<math><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>r</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>s</mi></mrow></mrow><mi>&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>N</mi></math> ...(i)

Where <math><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>r</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo><mi>&#160;</mi></mrow></mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow></math> ...(ii)

From (i) and (ii) we get,

<math><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>30</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></msqrt></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>rad</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q57. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The figure below shows a block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math> connected to an ideal string which passes through a thin fixed smooth pipe. On the other end, a particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is connected which revolves in a vertical circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math>. If the coefficient of friction between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math> and the surface is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>, then for what minimum value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math>, the block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> can undergo complete vertical circular motion?</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image1-c64b6a88-a88c-427e-8e34-89f4ed4f4d82.jpg" style="height: 125px; width: 200px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mi>min</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>m</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mi>min</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>m</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mi>min</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>m</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mi>min</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>15</mn><mi>m</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mi>min</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>m</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For the particle to undergo complete vertical circular motion, its speed at the lowest point should be at least <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>r</mi></msqrt></math>. Also, the tension in the string is maximum when the particle is at the lowest point. The tension at the lowest position of the particle is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></math></p><p>The friction force acting on the block should be able to balance the maximum tension.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>M</mi><mo>&#8805;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mi>min</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>m</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q58. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is suspended from a ceiling through a string of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math>. The particle moves in a horizontal circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math>. The speed (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math>) of the particle and the tension (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math>) in the string is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext>2</mtext><mi>r</mi><msqrt><mtext>g</mtext></msqrt></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2&#160;</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>  and  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext>3</mtext><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mi>L</mi></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi><msqrt><mtext>g</mtext></msqrt></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/4</mtext></msup></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mi>L</mi></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/3</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext>3</mtext><mi>r</mi><msqrt><mtext>g</mtext></msqrt></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2</mtext></msup></mfrac><mspace width="0.5em"/></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mspace width="0.5em"/><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mi>L</mi></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi><msqrt><mtext>g</mtext></msqrt></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/4</mtext></msup></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mi>L</mi></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi><msqrt><mtext>g</mtext></msqrt></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/4</mtext></msup></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mi>L</mi></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The situation is shown in the diagram. The angle &theta; made by the string with the vertical is given by

              <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mi>&#952;</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>r</mi><mi>L</mi></mfrac></math>                               ...(i)
 
<strong><u><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014019_AnsExp4_1.jpg" style="width: 196px; height: 295px;" /></u></strong>

The forces on the particle are

(1) the tension T along the string and

(2) the weight mg vertically downward.

The particle is moving in a circle with constant speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math>. Thus, the radial acceleration towards the centre has magnitude<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>r</mi></mfrac></math>. Resolving the forces along the radial direction and applying Newton&#39;s second law,

                 <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#8203;</mo><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo></math>              ...(ii)

As there is no acceleration in the vertical direction, net force in the vertical direction is zero,

             <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8203;</mo><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo></math>                    ...(iii)

Dividing (ii) by (iii),

<math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>tan</mtext> <mtext>&#952;</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>v</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>rg</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>or</mn> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>v</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mtext>rg</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>tan</mtext> <mtext>&#952;</mtext> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

And from (iii),

<math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>cos &#952;</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

Using (i),

<math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>v</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>r</mtext> <msqrt> <mtext>g</mtext> </msqrt> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>L</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mtext>r</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mtext>1/4</mtext> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>and</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>mgL</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>L</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mtext>r</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mtext>1/2</mtext> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>​

</details>

---

### Q59. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is whirled in a horizontal circle by means of a string at an initial speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math> revolutions per minute. Keeping the radius constant, if we want the tension in the string to be doubled, then the new speed should nearly be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rpm</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 118px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rpm</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 90px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi>rpm</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn><mo> </mo><mi>rpm</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>rpm</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 90px; top: -5.77778px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mtext>F</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>m</mtext> <msup> <mi>ω</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mr</mtext> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>n</mtext> <mi>π</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

thus <math> <mrow> <mtext>F</mtext> <mo>×</mo> <msup> <mtext>n</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>  n = resolution per second.

thus <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>F</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>F</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>n</mtext> <mo>′</mo> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>n</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

or   <math> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>n</mtext> <mo>′</mo> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mtext>n</mtext> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mn>5</mn> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>rpm</mtext> </mrow> </math>

= 7 rpm

</details>

---

### Q60. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A particle describes a horizontal circle in a conical funnel, whose inner surface is smooth, with a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0.5</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. What is the height (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cm</mi></math>) of the plane of the circle from the vertex of the funnel?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2.5**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/WhatsApp_Image_2020-07-27_at_3.36.40_PM.jpeg" style="width: 200px; height: 195px;" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi><mi>sin</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>r</mi></math>

Dividing above equations

 <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>tan</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>r</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfrac><mi>r</mi><mi>h</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>g</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

</details>

### Q61. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle moves in a circle with a uniform speed. When it goes from a point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> to a diametrically opposite point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, the momentum of the particle changes by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover><mtext>P</mtext><mo>→</mo></mover><mtext>A</mtext></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mover><mtext>P</mtext><mo>→</mo></mover><mtext>B</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mtext>j</mtext><mo>^</mo></mover><msup><mtext> kg m s</mtext><mn>-1</mn></msup></math>  and the centripetal force acting on it changes by <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>F</mtext> <mo>→</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mtext>A</mtext> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mrow> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>F</mtext> <mo>→</mo> </mover> </mrow> <mtext>B</mtext> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mn>8</mn> <mover accent="true"> <mtext>i</mtext> <mo>^</mo> </mover> <mtext> N</mtext> </mrow> </math>. Where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo mathvariant="normal">^</mo></mover></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> are unit vectors along <math><mi>x</mi></math> and <math><mi>y</mi></math> axes respectively. The angular velocity of the particle is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>8</mn></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>rad</mi></math> <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>4</mn></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>rad</mi></math> <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>16</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>rad</mi></math> <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>4</mn></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>rad</mi></math> <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover><mi>P</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>−</mo><mtext> </mtext><msub><mover><mi>P</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mover><mi>V</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mover><mi>V</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>−</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo>−</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mtext> </mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mtext> </mtext><mn>.....</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo></math>


<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover><mi>F</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mover><mi>F</mi><mo>→</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo>−</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>−</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo>+</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></mrow></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math> ...(ii)</p><p>Equating magnitudes (Also using the force relation given in question)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p><p>From the above two equations, we get

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>rad</mi></math> <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>
